| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | Support a MAC-CE in MSG4 PDSCH for triggering aperiodic SRS-AS/CSI-RS indicates at least<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic SRS-AS if triggered,<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic CSI-RS associated with AP CSI reporting if triggered, and<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of aperiodic CSI-RS of the first TRS burst, if triggered.<br>- The slot offset used to determine the transmission slot of the PUSCH for aperiodic CSI reporting, if triggered, via the UL grant provided in the MAC-CE<br>  - FFS: How to indicate the slot offset by the UL grant provided in the MAC-CE<br>Note: Whether to indicate the other settings (i.e., comb offset, repetition factor, cyclic shift and the number of TRS bursts, the parameters (e.g., slot offset, time gap) to determine the Tx slot(s) of the TRS burst(s), if triggered, other than the first TRS burst) via MAC-CE in MSG4 are separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | To determine the transmission/reception slot of the DL/UL signalling triggered/scheduled based on a legacy SCell activation command (i.e., aperiodic SRS-AS, aperiodic CSI-RS and the PUSCH scheduled for aperiodic CSI reporting), for applying a slot offset between a reference slot and a transmission/reception slot of the DL/UL signalling triggered/scheduled based on the legacy SCell activation command, the reference slot is the first slot that is 3ms after UE transmits HARQ-ACK for the PDSCH carrying the SCell activation command | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | To determine the transmission/reception slot of a MSG4-triggered DL/UL signalling (i.e., aperiodic SRS-AS, aperiodic TRS, aperiodic CSI-RS and the PUSCH scheduled for aperiodic CSI reporting), for applying a slot offset between a reference slot and a transmission/reception slot of the MSG4-triggered DL/UL signalling, the reference slot is the first slot that is 3ms after UE transmits HARQ-ACK for the MSG4 PDSCH carrying the MAC-CE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600647](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600647.zip) |
| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | For CSI-RS frequency-domain densities ρ = 1/3 and 1/6, support new CSI subband sizes as follows:<br>\| Bandwidth part size (PRBs) \| CSI subband size (PRBs) \|<br>\| 24-72 \| 6 (FFS: 12) \|<br>\| 73-144 \| 12 (FFS: 6) \|<br>\| 145-275 \| 24 (FFS: 30) \|<br>Note: Legacy PRG sizes are reused without enhancement. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Conclusion | No enhancement to the following legacy specification restriction on frequency density of CSI-RS port in a CSI subband for the frequency-domain densities ρ = 1/3 and 1/6, i.e., the following from Clause 5.2.1.4 in TS 38.214 shall be applied:<br>- A UE is not expected to be configured with csi-ReportingBand which contains a subband where a CSI-RS resource linked to the CSI Report setting has the frequency density of each CSI-RS port per PRB in the subband less than the configured density of the CSI-RS resource. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600648](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600648.zip) |
| RAN1#124 | 9.2.2 Enhancing DL CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | On triggering mechanism for early SRS/CSI-RS/CSI, via a DCI indicating switching out of dormancy for an SCell(s) based on legacy UE-specific DCI formats (e.g., DCI format 0_1/0_3/1_1/1_3),<br>- For triggering aperiodic SRS-AS, the SRS resource set(s) triggered for the SCell(s) is determined according to ‘SRS request’ field in the legacy DCI format 0_1/0_3/1_1/1_3 indicating switching out of dormancy for the SCell(s)<br>  - FFS: How to determine the serving cell(s) triggered with aperiodic SRS<br>  - FFS: How to coexist with legacy triggering mechanism for aperiodic SRS<br>- For triggering aperiodic CSI reporting and associated CSI-RS for CSI, the CSI report configuration(s) triggered for the SCell(s) is determined according to ‘CSI request’ field in the DCI formats 0_1/0_3 indicating switching out of dormancy for the SCell(s)<br>  - Note: UE determines the serving cell(s) which the associated CSI-RS is triggered for, via carrier configured in the CSI report configuration(s), where the CSI report configuration(s) is determined according to the ‘CSI request’ field.<br>  - Note: No additional RAN1 spec enhancement is needed to support aperiodic CSI reporting<br>Note: Legacy DCI format(s) for switching out of SCell dormancy is reused without introducing new DCI field or resizing the existing DCI field. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600649](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600649.zip) |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 Downlink transmission scheme(s) for downlink shared channels | 6G Study Item (6GR, 기타) | Agreement | - Study PDSCH and RS for PDSCH based on the following additional SLS EVM assumptions for HST scenario<br>  - Note: Other parameters follow agreements on SLS EVM for non-HST scenario<br>  - Note: LLS EVM parameters for HST scenario are separately discussed<br>\| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| Carrier frequency and duplex \| Around 4 GHz, TDD Around 7 GHz, TDD Around 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| Channel model \| TR 38.901 with spatial consistency (optional) \|<br>\| Scenario and deployment at around 4 and 7 GHz \| Rural macro, 500 m or 1732 m ISD between RRH sites, two TRxPs per RRH site, one or more RRH sites connected to a BBU, with RRH site to railway track distance: 100 m Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m \|<br>\| Scenario and deployment at around 30 GHz \| Rural macro, 500, 1732 m between BBU sites, one or more RRH sites connected to 1 BBU, one TRxP per RRH site, with RRH site to railway track distance: 5 m Inter-RRH site distance: [580 m, 580 m, 572 m] Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m \|<br>\| UE speed \| Rural macro: outdoor (350km/h, 500km/h) \|<br>\| Number of UEs per cell and UE distribution \| 300 (assuming 1000 passengers per train and at least 30% activity ratio) 100% of users in train Companies to report how UEs at distributed in train \|<br>\| Resource utilization \| 50%, 70%, 90% \|<br>\| Multi-TRP scenario \| Maximum number of coordinated TRPs is 2, 4, 8 or 16 Other number of coordinated TRPs are not precluded \|<br>\| CSI-RS periodicity \| 10 ms (optional) 20 ms (baseline) 40 ms (optional) 80 ms (optional) \|<br>\| BS transmit power (for 20 MHz) \| For around 0.7—7 GHz 49 dBm For around 30 GHz 33 dBm Note: BS Tx power scales up with bandwidth proportionally under the limitation of the maximum BS Tx power is 56 dBm Other BS transmit powers are not precluded (companies to report) \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

Note: other multi-TRP scenarios are not precluded Note: TRP assumption for HST scenarios will be treated separately \|<br>\| Multi-TRP Scenario 1: Intra-cell scenario, with 4 TRPs/RRHs per multi-TRP set \| Multi-TRP Scenario 2: Intra-site, inter-cell, with 3 TRPs per cooperative set (as baseline) \| Multi-TRP Scenario 3: Inter/intra-site, inter-cell with TRPs per cooperative set \|<br>\|  \|  \| Companies report the setting when N>1 \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

Others are not precluded. \|<br>\| #17 Link adaptation \| Based on CSI-RS (Adaptive Modulation and Coding, AMC) \|<br>\| #18 UE receiver type \| MMSE-IRC as baseline; other advanced receivers are not precluded \|<br>\| #19 BS Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #20 Maximum UE Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #21 Inter site distance \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 1732 m ISD \|<br>\| #22 UE antenna element radiation pattern \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #23 UE Speed and UE distribution \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 100% outdoor in train (up to 500km/h) \|<br>\| #24 UE rotation \| No UE rotation (baseline). Each sector is a cell and that the cell association is geometry based. Company to report whether inter-cell handover or switching is disabled during the simulation. Company to report whether the trajectory should be terminated (e.g., for co-located case) or be retained (e.g., non co-located case), if the UE trajectory hits the site boundary (the hexagonal grid) Other UE mobility model(s), e.g., EVM for NR ICBM, are not precluded (companies to report). \|<br>\| #30 Scheduling algorithm \| PF scheduler \|<br>\| #31 MCS \| Use NR MCS \|<br>\| #32 Traffic Model \| FTP model 1/3 with packet size 0.5 Mbytes (other value is not precluded) Other traffic models are not precluded (companies to report). Full buffer (Optional) \|<br>\| #33 CSI Feedback \| CSI periodicity: 10ms, 20ms (baseline), 40ms, 80ms, 160ms FFS: Other update for high-speed train \|<br>\| #34 Resource Utilization \| 30%, 50%, 70% (optional) \|<br>\| #35 Prediction parameter \| Company can report assumption for set-A and set-B, prediction/observation window and any extension for multi-TRP measurement Set A: {16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024}. Other values are not precluded. Set B: {4, 8, 16, 32, 48, 64}. Other values are not precluded: Set A/Set B combinations: Alt1: Set B is different from Set A Alt2: Set B is a subset of Set A Alt3: Set B is the same as Set A Regarding temporal domain prediction, the following can be considered: Time gap between predicted time instances (including first future time instance): {40ms, 80ms, 120ms, 160ms, 200ms}. Other values not precluded Prediction/observation window: {1, 2, 4, 8} predicted/measured time instances. Other values not precluded. Note: Regarding cross-frequency prediction, as a baseline, NW antenna(s) for source and target frequencies reuse the same placement and orientation. Others are not precluded (companies to report). \|<br>\| #36 UE number/cell \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #37 Layout/deployment \| Reuse the assumptions of uplink/downlink transmission schemes (10.5.2.2/3) 7 x 3, single layer, hex grid (baseline) 7 x 3 two layers, macro layer: hex grid; micro layer: locations of micro sites are reported by company (optional) 19 x 3, single layer, hex grid (optional) FFS: Other update for high-speed train \|<br>\| #38 Inter-cell interference model \| Reuse the assumptions of DL-based CSI (10.5.3.1) Explicitly and realistically modelled \|<br>\| #39 Metric \| Top-K/1 beam prediction accuracy (with or without L1-RSRP margin), average UPT, and 5%-ile UPT Companies can report other performance metrics observations, e.g., SINR/RSRP prediction accuracy, RS overhead reduction, beam management latency, model and computational complexity, etc. Companies report the assumption of transmission scheme (e.g., Type-I/II PMI or SVD), if UPT is reported. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | For spatial domain CSI prediction, Baseline #2 is Linear interpolation, where the channel of an unmeasured port uses the channel of the nearest measured ports based on CSI-RS port indexing (for evaluation purpose, 5G NR port indexing is assumed) in the same polarization, without using assistance information derived from full-dimensional measurement.<br>- Companies to report remaining details if any, including other baselines | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | For CSI-RS for CSI acquisition in 6GR, study the maximum number of ports considering the following alternatives<br>  - Alt 1: The maximum number of CSI-RS ports is 128<br>  - Alt 2: The maximum number of CSI-RS ports is 256<br>  - Alt 3: The maximum number of CSI-RS ports is 512<br>  - The study includes evaluation of the performance and CSI-RS overhead<br>    - Note: relevant complexity can also be considered<br>  - FFS whether all the NR port numbers are supported | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | 6GR supports NZP CSI-RS as the channel measurement RS for DL based CSI acquisition<br>- FFS other reference signals, e.g., DMRS<br>- FFS Aspects related with non-precoded CSI-RS or precoded CSI-RS | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | Consider CSI-IM as the baseline for interference measurement resource for DL CSI acquisition in 6GR<br>- FFS other RS types, e.g., NZP CSI-RS, DMRS, etc | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | On time-domain type of 6G CSI-RS for downlink based CSI acquisition, at least support periodic and aperiodic CSI-RS, and further study<br>- Whether semi-persistent CSI-RS is additionally supported<br>- Whether new time-domain behavior is beneficial | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | 6GR CSI-RS pattern is defined as aggregation of CDM groups, for which study the following aspects<br>- Whether CSI-RS pattern is defined on single or multiple slots/RBs in time/frequency domain<br>- For CDM design, consider the following options<br>  - RE locations of CDM groups<br>    - Option 1-1: Localized CDM group similar to 5G NR, where one CDM group contains adjacent REs<br>    - Option 1-2: Comb-based CDM group, where subcarriers in one CDM group are distributed following comb pattern<br>  - CDM group size and type<br>    - Option 2-1: Reuse/Simplify NR, i.e., CDM 1, 2, 4, 8 with potential reduction of candidates,<br>    - Option 2-2: Larger CDM size, e.g., CDM 16<br>    - Option 2-3: New CDM type for NR CDM size (e.g., fd-CDM4 or cdm8-FD4-TD2)<br>- At least following aspects to be considered for designing CSI-RS pattern<br>  - CSI-RS Overhead<br>  - CSI-RS coverage<br>  - CSI-RS sharing use cases<br>  - Channel estimation performance<br>  - NES<br>  - Coexistence with other signals/channels<br>  - Digital precoding/Hybrid precoding structures<br>  - MRSS | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | For the study of non-AI/AI based time domain CSI prediction at UE or NW side, consider<br>- Intermediate metric: Subband-level per layer per predicted time instance eigenvector/precoder SGCS, NMSE calculated between predicted channel and ground-truth channel<br>  - To compare NW side prediction with UE side prediction, the impact of CSI reporting shall be considered<br>  - FFS WB SGCS/NMSE<br>- System-level metric: UPT (mean, 5%tile), CSI reporting overhead<br>  - CSI-RS overhead is considered in UPT calculation<br>  - To evaluate CSI report overhead, companies to report how the PMI/channel of the predicted time instance(s) is reported<br>- Link-level metric: BLER/SE/throughput<br>- Complexity metrics: FLOPs/M<br>  - For AI based prediction: number of parameters/M<br>- Baseline: Sample & Hold<br>- Additional EVM assumption<br>  - Channel modelling: Spatial consistency with mobility procedure A<br>  - UE distribution: 100% outdoors with speed 10, 20, 30, 60, 120km/h<br>    - Other speeds are not precluded<br>  - UE Rx antenna phase discontinuity modelling: Companies to report the used model<br>  - Observation window (number/distance between adjacent CSI-RS occasions): e.g., 5/10ms, 5/20ms for periodic CSI-RS; and 12/2ms, 8/2ms, 4/2ms for aperiodic CSI-RS;<br>    - other values can be additionally submitted<br>  - Prediction window (number/length of each predicted instances/gap from the last observation instance to the 1st predicted instance):  e.g., 1/10ms/5ms, 4/10ms/5ms;<br>    - other values can be additionally submitted. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.3.1 Aspects of downlink-based CSI acquisition | CSI Acquisition / Prediction | Agreement | For 6GR CSI-RS for DL based CSI acquisition, study CSI-RS RE sharing considering the following use cases.<br>- Use case 1: Sharing a common set or subset of REs among CSI-RS with different number of ports<br>- Use case 2: Sharing a common set or subset of REs among CSI-RS with different frequency densities<br>- Use case 3: Sharing a common set or subset of REs among CSI-RS with different periodicities/time domain types<br>- Use case 4: Sharing a common set or subset of REs among 5G CSI-RS and 6G CSI-RS<br>- Note: the combination of the above use cases can be considered.<br>- Note: the above use cases are design principles at this stage. Whether there is specification impact subjects to further discussion.<br>- Note: the scenarios for sharing of CSI-RS may need to consider CSI-RS channel estimation performance at cell-edge, precoded/non-precoded CSI-RS, AI/non-AI, NES enabled/disabled, UEs with different measurement capabilities, single-TRP/Panel or multi-TRP/Panel etc | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 8.1 Maintenance on Artificial Intelligence (AI)/Machine Learning (ML) for NR Air Interface | Rel-19 Maintenance (AI/ML, MIMO Ph5, NES, NTN 등) | Agreement | The following TP for TS 38.214 Clause 5.2.1.6 is endorsed.<br>The corresponding final CR for Rel-19 TS 38.214 in R1-2603379 is endorsed<br>Reason for change: CPU duration for beam management related UE-side data collection is not captured correctly. In current specification, periodic CSI report for UE-side data collection for beam management is not supported.<br>\| Agreement For UE-sided model, regarding the resource type for data collection purpose, only always-on SSB and P/SP CSI-RS are supported. Agreement For UE-sided model, regarding a CSI-ReportConfig for data collection, Reuse the existing CPU occupation time for a CSI report with CSI-ReportConfig with reportQuantity set to 'none' and TRS-info not configured \|<br>Summary of change: Reflect the RAN1 agreements accurately in Section 5.2.1.6 of TS 38.214. Include periodic CSI report for UE-side data collection for beam management.<br>Consequence if not approved: Incorrect UE behaviour for CPU duration for UE-side data collection for training.<br>\| 5.2.1.6 CSI processing criteria < Unchanged parts are omitted > For a CSI report with CSI-ReportConfig with higher layer parameter reportQuantity set to 'none' and CSI-RS-ResourceSet with higher layer parameter trs-Info not configured, or reportQuantity set to 'none-bm-r19', the CPU(s) are occupied for a number of OFDM symbols as follows: - A semi-persistent CSI report (excluding an initial semi-persistent CSI report on PUSCH after the PDCCH triggering the report) occupies CPU(s) from the first symbol of the earliest one of each transmission occasion of periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS/SSB resource for channel measurement for L1-RSRP computation, until symbols after the last symbol of the latest one of the CSI-RS/SSB resource for channel measurement for L1-RSRP computation in each transmission occasion. - An aperiodic CSI report occupies CPU(s) from the first symbol after the PDCCH triggering the CSI report until the last symbol between symbols after the first symbol after the PDCCH triggering the CSI report and symbols after the last symbol of the latest one of each CSI-RS/SSB resource for channel measurement for L1-RSRP computation. For a CSI report with CSI-ReportConfig with higher layer parameter reportQuantity set to 'none-csi-r19', the CPU(s) are occupied for a number of OFDM symbols as follows: - CSI report occupies CPU(s) from the first symbol of each transmission occasion of periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS resource for channel measurement, until symbols after the transmission occasion of periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS resource for channel measurement. where are defined in the table 5.4-2. For a CSI report with CSI-ReportConfig with higher layer parameter reportQuantity set to 'none-bm-r19', the CPU(s) are occupied for a number of OFDM symbols as follows: - CSI report occupies CPU(s) from the first symbol of the earliest one of each transmission occasion of periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS/SSB resource for channel measurement for L1-RSRP computation, until symbols after the last symbol of the latest one of the CSI-RS/SSB resource for channel measurement for L1-RSRP computation in each transmission occasion. where are defined in the table 5.4-2. < Unchanged parts are omitted > \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603260](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603260.zip)<br>`[124b-R19-AI/ML]` |

final CR for Rel-19 TS38.212 in R1-2603414 is endorsed.<br>\| Impacted spec(s): \|<br>\| Reason for change: Per 38.214, for RRC connected mode, a UE is configured with NZP-CSI-RS-ResourceSet with Consequences if not approved: Inconsistent referencing and ambiguous UE behavior on which resource set index to include in the CSI report for CJT delay and frequency offsets. \|<br>\| 6.3.2.1.2 CSI -------------------------------------------Unchanged parts are omitted------------------------------------------ Table 6.3.2.1.2-3E: Mapping order of CSI fields of one CSI report for reportQuantity=cjtc-Dd Table 6.3.2.1.2-3F: Mapping order of CSI fields of one CSI report for reportQuantity=cjtc-F Table 6.3.2.1.2-3G: Mapping order of CSI fields of one CSI report for reportQuantity=cjtc-Dd-F -------------------------------------------Unchanged parts are omitted------------------------------------------ Table 6.3.2.1.2-11: Reference TRS resource set index of reference CSI-RS resource set for tracking, delay offset and inside/outside indicator for reportQuantity=cjtc-Dd -------------------------------------------Unchanged parts are omitted------------------------------------------ Table 6.3.2.1.2-12: Reference TRS resource set index of reference CSI-RS resource set for tracking, and frequency offset for reportQuantity=cjtc-F \| | RAN1#124bis | 8.3 Maintenance on Evolution of NR duplex operation: Sub-band full duplex (SBFD) | Rel-19 Maintenance (AI/ML, MIMO Ph5, NES, NTN 등) | Agreement | Adopt the following TPs for TS38.214 and TS38.213.<br>\| 6.2.1.5 UE sounding procedure for SBFD *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** For a periodic or semi-persistent SRS resource, UE doesn’t transmit an SRS transmission occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>\| Reason for change Current specification is missing the implementation of RAN1 agreement that UE should drop SRS transmission occasion that is mapped to SBFD and non-SBFD symbols. Without this correction, the specification gives a wrong understanding for UE transmission behavior for an SRS transmission occasion across mixed symbol type, in which UE should transmit SRS only in the valid symbol type. Summary of change Capture UE behavior for a periodic or semi-persistent SRS resource in which the UE doesn’t transmit the SRS transmission occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. \|<br>\| 5.2.1.4.1 Resource Setting configuration *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** For a CSI report associated with semi-persistent or periodic CSI-RS, if a UE is configured with SBFD symbols and a CSI-ReportConfig with the higher layer parameter symbolType, the UE only considers the CSI-RS occasions within either SBFD symbol(s) or non-SBFD symbol(s) as indicated by symbolType for the CSI derivation for the CSI report. The symbolType configured in CSI-ReportConfig applies to NZP-CSI-RS resources that are associated with the CSI report and configured for both channel measurement and interference measurement. For a periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS, UE doesn’t receive the CSI-RS reception occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>\| Reason for change Current specification is missing the implementation of RAN1 agreement that UE should drop CSI-RS reception occasion that is mapped to SBFD and non-SBFD symbols. Summary of change Capture UE behavior for a periodic or semi-persistent CSI-RS resource in which the UE doesn’t receive the CSI-RS reception occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. \|<br>\| 11.1 Slot configuration *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** Except for a PRACH transmission in a PRACH occasion determined based on a second RACH configuration provided by sbfd-RACHDualConfig as described in clause 8, the UE transmits or receives a physical channel or signal either only in SBFD symbols or only in non-SBFD symbols. For a PUCCH transmission with SP-CSI reports, or for a PUCCH with SR, LRR, UEIRI, UE doesn’t transmit the PUCCH transmission occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. *** Unchanged parts are omitted *** \|<br>\| Reason for change Current specification is missing the implementation of RAN1 agreement that UE should drop P/SP PUCCH transmission occasion that is mapped to SBFD and non-SBFD symbols. Summary of change Capture UE behavior for a periodic or semi-persistent PUCCH transmission occasion in which the UE doesn’t transmit the PUCCH occasion that is mapped to SBFD symbols and non-SBFD symbols. \|<br>Final CRs in R1-2603431 and R1-2603468 are endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603361](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603361.zip)<br>`[124b-R19-SBFD]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 8.5 Maintenance on Low-power wake-up signal and receiver for NR (LP-WUS/WUR) | Rel-19 Maintenance (AI/ML, MIMO Ph5, NES, NTN 등) | Agreement | Adopt the following TP to section 10.4D, TS 38.213 as alignment CR:<br>\| ======================== Start of TP======================================= 10.4D PDCCH monitoring activation by WUS in RRC_CONNECTED A UE configured with DRX mode operation and operating in the RRC_CONNECTED state can be provided for WUS reception on the primary cell of a cell group - a number of OOK symbols per OFDM symbol, a first RB by lpwus-StartRB, and overlaid OFDM sequences per OOK symbol for WUS reception [4, TS 38.211], and - a number of codepoints provided for the UE by the WUS [6, TS 38.212], by lpwus-CodepointWUS-codepointCONNECTED A UE assumes that a WUS is quasi co-located with an SS/PBCH block or a CSI-RS with respect to quasi co-location ‘typeC’ or ‘typeD’ properties, when applicable. If a UE is provided dl-OrJointTCI-StateList, the UE receives WUS based on the quasi co-location information of the TCI states provided by dl-OrJointTCI-StateList, indicated by a most recent DCI format or MAC CE, after a respective application time in clause 5.1.5 [6, TS 38.214]; otherwise, the UE receives WUS based on the quasi co-location information of the TCI states for a CORESET with controlResourceSetId value that is same as the one indicated by lpwus-TCI-StatesWUS_TCI_states_CONNECTED. A UE assumes that an SCS configuration for WUS receptions is same as an SCS configuration for the active DL BWP. A UE does not monitor a WUS during Active Time [11, TS 38.321]. A UE does not monitor WUS during DTX inactive period for the primary cell. When lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer is not provided, A a UE can be provided by lpwus-MO-1-1WUS-MOCONNECTED-Option1-1 a periodicity, by periodicityMO-Option 1-1, and a time offset, by offsetMO-Option 1-1, relative to the start of a system frame with SFN 0, for the UE to determine WUS monitoring occasions. The UE starts to monitor WUS in a first WUS monitoring occasion that is not earlier than a first slot that is prior to a second slot where the drx-onDurationTimer would start by a time provided by lpwus-TimeOffset1-1timeOffsetCONNECTEDOption1-1, and monitors WUS for a number of monitoring occasions provided by lpwus-NumOfMO-1-1numMO-Option 1-1. The UE reports a number of milliseconds [18, TS 38.306] where the UE is not required to monitor WUS prior to the slot where the drx-onDurationTimer would start. The UE is not required to monitor WUS within the reported number of slots prior to the slot where the drx-onDurationTimer would start. If the UE determines to monitor PDCCH based on a detected WUS, the UE starts the drx-onDurationTimer [11, TS 38.321]. When lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer is provided, aA UE can be provided by lpwus-MO-1-2WUS-MOCONNECTED-Option1-2 a periodicity, by periodicityMO-Option 1-2, and a time offset, by offsetMO-Option 1-2, relative to the start of a system frame with SFN 0, for the UE to determine first WUS monitoring occasions from a number of WUS monitoring occasions per periodicity, provided by lpwus-NumOfMO-1-2numMO-perPeriodicity-Option 1-2. The UE assumes WUS monitoring occasions per periodicity are consecutive in time. The UE reports a number of milliseconds [18, TS 38.306] and expects that a time gap, from a last WUS monitoring occasion from the number of WUS monitoring occasions per periodicity to the slot where the lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer would start, is no smaller than the reported number of milliseconds If the UE determines to monitor PDCCH based on a detected WUS, the UE starts lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer [11, TS 38.321] after a time, provided by lpwus-TimeOffset1-2timeOffsetCONNECTEDOption1-2, with respect to the start of the first WUS monitoring occasion from the number of WUS monitoring occasions per periodicity. A UE can be provided, by lpwus-AvailableSlot in LPWUS-ConfigWUS_available_slot_CONNECTED, a bitmap that corresponds to a set of time units that repeats continuously and indicates a subset of time units from the set of time units that is available for the UE to monitor WUS [12, TS 38.331]. A time unit includes one slot or two slots. A set of time units includes a total of either 10, or 20, or 40 time units. A duration , in msec, of the set of time units has maximum value of 40 msec. The first symbol of the set of time units every 40 msec/ periods is a first symbol in frame mod 4 = 0. The UE can be additionally provided, by lpwus-AvailableSymbol in LPWUS-ConfigWUS_available_symbol_CONNECTED, an indication of symbols in each time unit from the subset of time units that is available for the UE to monitor WUS. If the UE is not provided lpwus-AvailableSlotWUS_available_slot_CONNECTED, the UE assumes that all time units are available for the UE to monitor WUS. If the UE is not provided lpwus-AvailableSymbolWUS_available_symbol_CONNECTED, the UE assumes a time unit of one slot and, for a time unit that is available for the UE to monitor WUS, all symbols in the time unit are available for the UE to monitor WUS. The UE assumes that a symbol is not available to monitor WUS when - the symbol is indicated as uplink, by tdd-UL-DL-configurationCommon or tdd-UL-DL-ConfigurationDedicated - the symbol is indicated for transmission of SS/PBCH blocks, by ssb-PositionsInBurst in SIB1 or in ServingCellConfigCommon A WUS monitoring occasion is over a first number of symbols, provided by lpwus-NominalMoDuration in LPWUS-ConfigWUS_NominalMO_duration_CONNECTED. If a number of available symbols for the UE to monitor WUS in a WUS monitoring occasion is smaller than a second number of symbols, provided by lpwus-ActualDuration in LPWUS-ConfigWUS_ActualMO_duration_CONNECTED, the UE does not monitor WUS in the WUS monitoring occasion. The UE monitors WUS in a WUS monitoring occasion over the earliest available lpwus-ActualDurationWUS_ActualMO_duration_CONNECTED symbols in the WUS monitoring occasion. If a UE detects a codepoint in a WUS reception, from the number of codepoints, on the primary cell of the cell group, the UE starts monitoring PDCCH on all applicable serving cells of the cell group, and the UE does not need to monitor WUS before lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer starts, if lpwus-PDCCH-MonitoringTimer is provided. ========================= End of TP======================================= \|<br>R1-263402	Summary #2 of maintenance on Low-power wake-up signal and receiver for NR (LP-WUS/WUR)			Moderator (vivo) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602036](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602036.zip)<br>`[124b-R19-LP-WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 8.7 Maintenance on others | Rel-19 Maintenance (AI/ML, MIMO Ph5, NES, NTN 등) | Agreement | - Adopt the following TP to TS38.214 Section 5.2.4a<br>\| Reason for change: periodic CSI-RS/CSI-IM configuration for early CSI acquisition for a candidate cell is captured in the TS 38.214, while the semi-persistent CSI-RS/CSI-IM support is missing. Summary of change: Clarify that the CSI-RS/CSI-IM for CSI acquisition on a candidate cell can be semi-persistently activated. Consequences if not approved: UE’s behaviors of CSI acquisition for a candidate cell become incomplete. \|<br>\| 5.2.4a CSI Reporting for LTM and handover <Unchanged parts are omitted> After a UE receives an LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE [10, TS 38.321] providing a candidate cell (given by Target Configuration ID field), and a ltm-CSI-ReportConfig is configured for the candidate cell, the UE can measure corresponding NZP CSI-RS resources and CSI-IM resources if configured, and shall transmit a CSI report to the candidate cell. After a UE receives a ReconfigurationWithSync configured with earlyCSI-Acquisition, the UE can measure corresponding NZP CSI-RS resources and CSI-IM resources if configured, and shall transmit a CSI report to the target cell. <Unchanged parts are omitted> If a valid CSI is not available, the UE shall transmit a CSI report which contains a CQI corresponding to the lowest CQI index. Depending on the UE capability, the UE may measure NZP CSI-RS resources and CSI-IM resources if configured corresponding to a ltm-CSI-ReportConfig before receiving the LTM Cell Switch Command MAC CE [10, TS 38.321]. \|<br>The final CR in R1-2603406 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603335](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603335.zip)<br>`[124b-R19-others]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.2.3 Uplink transmission scheme(s) for uplink channels | 6G Study Item (6GR, 기타) | Agreement | Study DL/UL reciprocity based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, considering at least following candidate schemes:<br>- Scheme 1. CSI-RS based DL channel estimation by UE, UE calculates UL precoder, UE transmits (precoded) SRS, and NW indicates UE precoder information to schedule (e.g., analogous with non-codebook based PUSCH in NR)<br>- Scheme 2. CSI-RS-based DL channel acquisition by UE, UE reports channel information to NW, and NW selects an UL precoder to schedule UE<br>  - FFS: What channel information UE reports<br>  - FFS: how to determine UL precoder<br>  - FFS: UL link adaptation<br>- Scheme 3. CSI-RS-based DL channel acquisition by UE, UE reports channel information to NW and/or send SRS, the NW indicates the information on the desired UL precoder, the UE calculates/selects the precoder<br>  - FFS: What channel information UE reports<br>  - FFS: How NW indicates the information on the desired UL precoder<br>  - FFS: How to represent the information<br>  - FFS: How the UE calculates/selects the precoder.<br>- Other scheme(s) are not precluded.<br>- Note: For any scheme(s) requiring DL/UL reciprocity at UE side, the feasibility of DL/UL reciprocity at UE side should be considered.<br>- Potential benefit(s) & scenario(s) over codebook-based PUSCH transmission as baseline<br>For the purpose of performance evaluation of DL/UL reciprocity based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, consider the following antenna calibration error:<br>- Antenna calibration error can be modeled as<br>  - represents the uplink channel obtained using DL/UL reciprocity at UE side and has a dimension .<br>  - is the downlink channel derived by CSI-RS and has a dimension .<br>  - is the number of antenna ports of UE,  is the number antenna ports of aNB.<br>  - is the amplitude mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance X1=0.35 dB.<br>  - is the phase mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance Y1=5 degrees.<br>  - Other values on X1 and Y1 can be considered and reported by companies.<br>  - is the amplitude mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance X2 dB.<br>  - is the phase mismatch coefficient and could be modeled as subject to uniform or Gaussian distribution, e.g., Gaussian distribution with zero mean and variance Y2 degrees.<br>  - Values on X2 and Y2 are reported by companies.<br>  - The distribution of  and  can also be reported by companies.<br>  - Both or either of aNB or UE-side antenna calibration error can be used in the simulation and is reported by companies.<br>  - It is assumed that DL/UL reciprocity calibration error is constant in UL scheduled bandwidth.<br>  - Companies report how DL/UL reciprocity calibration error behavior in time domain considering UL coherency is modeled.<br>- Send LS to RAN4 to inform the above contents and ask for feedback on model assumption above, whether DL/UL reciprocity can be achieved at UE side considering different UE implementations<br>- RAN1 continue to study, and after RAN1 sensitivity check, send an LS to RAN4 to inform the above and the outcome, with potential assessment/check from RAN4 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.2.4 Beam management for downlink and uplink | Beam Management | Agreement | Support the following table as the assumption of SLS for evaluation of 6GR beam management<br>- Revise the RAN1#124-bis agreement as follows:<br>\| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| #1 Scenario (Carrier frequency) \| Indoor hotspot, Urban macro, Dense urban (one-layer, and two-layer), High Speed Train, SMa, Urban grid, 4 GHz, TDD 7 GHz, TDD 30 GHz, TDD Other scenarios are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| #2 Mode \| DL/UL SU-MIMO/MU-MIMO \|<br>\| #3 System BW \| 4GHz: 20MHz 7GHz: 100 MHz 30GHz: 100 MHz \|<br>\| #4 Subcarrier spacing for data \| 4GHz/7GHz: 30KHz; 30GHz: 120 KHz \|<br>\| #5 Channel model \| TR 38.901 with the following features, if needed: Spatial consistency, including Autocorrelation of shadow fading (Section 7.4.4 in TR 38.901) Correlation modeling for multi‑frequency simulations. Others are not precluded (companies to report) \|<br>\| #16 Beam correspondence \| Ideal (baseline) or non-ideal. Others are not precluded. \|<br>\| #17 Link adaptation \| Based on CSI-RS (Adaptive Modulation and Coding, AMC) \|<br>\| #18 UE receiver type \| MMSE-IRC as baseline; other advanced receivers are not precluded \|<br>\| #19 BS Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #20 Maximum UE Tx Power \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #21 Inter site distance \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 1732 m ISD \|<br>\| #22 UE antenna element radiation pattern \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| #23 UE Speed and UE distribution \| Reuse the configuration(s) from 6GR air interface evaluation assumptions, besides for the following: High Speed Train: 100% outdoor in train (up to 500km/h) \|<br>\| #24 UE rotation \| No UE rotation (baseline). UE rotation is not precluded and the corresponding rotation speed is up to companies to report. \|<br>\| #25 BS Antenna height \| Dense urban macro and Urban Macro: 25 m; Dense urban micro: 10 m; Indoor: 3m \|<br>\| #26 UE Antenna height \| Section 7.2 in TR 38.901.
